<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Andorra&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Schools">Schools</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Andorra&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Schools">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Children between the ages of 6 and 16 are required by law to have full-time education. Education up to secondary level is provided free of charge by the government.
</p><p>There are three systems of school, Andorran, French and Spanish, which use Catalan, French and Spanish languages respectively, as the main language of instruction. Parents may choose which system their children attend. All schools are built and maintained by Andorran authorities, but teachers in the French and Spanish schools are paid for the most part by France and Spain. 39% of Andorran children attend Andorran schools, 33% attend French schools, and 28% Spanish schools.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="University_of_Andorra">University of Andorra</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Andorra&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: University of Andorra">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Universitat_d%27Andorra" title="Universitat d&#39;Andorra">Universitat d'Andorra</a> (UdA) is the state public university and is the only university in Andorra. It was established in 1997. The university provides first-level degrees in nursing, computer science, business administration, and educational sciences, in addition to higher professional education courses. The only two graduate schools in Andorra are the Nursing School and the School of Computer Science, the latter having a PhD programme.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Virtual_Studies_Centre">Virtual Studies Centre</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Andorra&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Virtual Studies Centre">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The geographical complexity of the country as well as the small number of students prevents the University of Andorra from developing a full academic programme, and it serves principally as a centre for virtual studies, connected to Spanish and French universities. The Virtual Studies Centre (Centre d'Estudis Virtuals) at the university runs approximately 20 different academic degrees at both undergraduate and postgraduate levels in fields including tourism, law, Catalan <a href="/wiki/Philology" title="Philology">philology</a>, humanities, psychology, political sciences, audiovisual communication, telecommunications engineering, and East Asia studies. The centre also runs various postgraduate programmes and continuing-education courses for professionals.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Andorra&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Transport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Andorra" title="Transport in Andorra">Transport in Andorra</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Andorra%E2%80%93La_Seu_d%27Urgell_Airport" title="Andorra–La Seu d&#39;Urgell Airport">Andorra–La Seu d'Urgell Airport</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pirineus_-_la_Seu_d%27Urgell_airport.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e1/Pirineus_-_la_Seu_d%27Urgell_airport.jpg/220px-Pirineus_-_la_Seu_d%27Urgell_airport.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="171" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e1/Pirineus_-_la_Seu_d%27Urgell_airport.jpg/330px-Pirineus_-_la_Seu_d%27Urgell_airport.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e1/Pirineus_-_la_Seu_d%27Urgell_airport.jpg/440px-Pirineus_-_la_Seu_d%27Urgell_airport.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3244" data-file-height="2518" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pirineus_-_la_Seu_d%27Urgell_airport.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Andorra%E2%80%93La_Seu_d%27Urgell_Airport" title="Andorra–La Seu d&#39;Urgell Airport">Andorra–La Seu d'Urgell Airport</a>, located 12&#160;km away from Andorra, in <a href="/wiki/Montferrer_i_Castellb%C3%B2" title="Montferrer i Castellbò">Montferrer i Castellbò</a> (<a href="/wiki/Catalonia" title="Catalonia">Catalonia</a>, Eastern <a href="/wiki/Spain" title="Spain">Spain</a>)</div></div></div>
<p>Until the 20th century, Andorra had very limited transport links to the outside world, and development of the country was affected by its physical isolation. Even now, the nearest major airports at Toulouse and Barcelona are both three hours' drive from Andorra.
</p><p>Andorra has a road network of 279&#160;km (173&#160;mi), of which 76&#160;km (47&#160;mi) is unpaved. The two main roads out of <a href="/wiki/Andorra_la_Vella" title="Andorra la Vella">Andorra la Vella</a> are the CG-1 to the <a href="/wiki/Andorra%E2%80%93Spain_border" title="Andorra–Spain border">Spanish border</a> near <a href="/wiki/Sant_Juli%C3%A0_de_L%C3%B2ria" title="Sant Julià de Lòria">Sant Julià de Lòria</a>, and the CG-2 to the <a href="/wiki/Andorra%E2%80%93France_border" title="Andorra–France border">French border</a> via the Envalira Tunnel near <a href="/wiki/El_Pas_de_la_Casa" title="El Pas de la Casa">El Pas de la Casa</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-mobi_198-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mobi-198">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup> Bus services cover all metropolitan areas and many rural communities, with services on most major routes running half-hourly or more frequently during peak travel times. There are frequent long-distance bus services from Andorra to <a href="/wiki/Barcelona" title="Barcelona">Barcelona</a> and <a href="/wiki/Toulouse" title="Toulouse">Toulouse</a>, plus a daily tour from the former city. Bus services mostly are run by private companies, but some local ones are operated by the government.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:AGC_Z_27500.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/AGC_Z_27500.jpg/220px-AGC_Z_27500.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="110" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/AGC_Z_27500.jpg/330px-AGC_Z_27500.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/AGC_Z_27500.jpg/440px-AGC_Z_27500.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3000" data-file-height="1500" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:AGC_Z_27500.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A train at <a href="/wiki/Latour-de-Carol" title="Latour-de-Carol">Latour-de-Carol</a> (La Tor de Querol), one of the two stations serving Andorra. Andorra has no railways, although the line connecting Latour-de-Carol and <a href="/wiki/Toulouse" title="Toulouse">Toulouse</a>, which in turn connects to France's <a href="/wiki/TGV" title="TGV">TGVs</a> at Toulouse, runs within two kilometres (1.2 miles) of the Andorran border.</div></div></div>
<p>There are no airports for fixed-wing aircraft within Andorra's borders but there are, however, heliports in <a href="/wiki/La_Massana" title="La Massana">La Massana</a> (Camí Heliport), <a href="/wiki/Arinsal" title="Arinsal">Arinsal</a> and <a href="/wiki/Escaldes-Engordany" class="mw-redirect" title="Escaldes-Engordany">Escaldes-Engordany</a> with commercial helicopter services<sup id="cite_ref-heliand_199-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-heliand-199">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-helitrans_200-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-helitrans-200">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup> and an airport located in the neighbouring Spanish comarca of <a href="/wiki/Alt_Urgell" title="Alt Urgell">Alt Urgell</a>, 12 kilometres (7.5 miles) south of the Andorran-Spanish border.<sup id="cite_ref-201" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-201">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup> Since July 2015, <a href="/wiki/Andorra%E2%80%93La_Seu_d%27Urgell_Airport" title="Andorra–La Seu d&#39;Urgell Airport">Andorra–La Seu d'Urgell Airport</a> has operated commercial flights to <a href="/wiki/Adolfo_Su%C3%A1rez_Madrid%E2%80%93Barajas_Airport" class="mw-redirect" title="Adolfo Suárez Madrid–Barajas Airport">Madrid</a> and <a href="/wiki/Palma_de_Mallorca_Airport" title="Palma de Mallorca Airport">Palma de Mallorca</a>, and is the <a href="/wiki/Airline_hub" title="Airline hub">main hub</a> for <a href="/wiki/Air_Andorra" title="Air Andorra">Air Andorra</a> and <a href="/wiki/Andorra_Airlines" title="Andorra Airlines">Andorra Airlines</a>. As of 11 July 2018, there are no regular commercial flights at the airport.
</p><p>Nearby airports located in Spain and France provide access to international flights for the principality. The nearest airports are at <a href="/wiki/Perpignan%E2%80%93Rivesaltes_Airport" title="Perpignan–Rivesaltes Airport">Perpignan</a>, France (156 kilometres or 97 miles from Andorra) and <a href="/wiki/Lleida-Alguaire_Airport" class="mw-redirect" title="Lleida-Alguaire Airport">Lleida</a>, Spain (160 kilometres or 99 miles from Andorra). The largest nearby airports are at Toulouse, France (165 kilometres or 103 miles from Andorra) and Barcelona, Spain (215 kilometres or 134 miles from Andorra). There are hourly bus services from both Barcelona and Toulouse airports to Andorra.
</p><p>The nearest railway station is <a href="/wiki/Andorre-L%27Hospitalet_station" title="Andorre-L&#39;Hospitalet station">Andorre-L'Hospitalet station</a> 10&#160;km (6&#160;mi) east of Andorra which is on the <span class="nowrap">1,435&#160;mm</span> (<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1020198016">.mw-parser-output .frac{white-space:nowrap}.mw-parser-output .frac .num,.mw-parser-output .frac .den{font-size:80%;line-height:0;vertical-align:super}.mw-parser-output .frac .den{vertical-align:sub}.mw-parser-output .sr-only{border:0;clip:rect(0,0,0,0);height:1px;margin:-1px;overflow:hidden;padding:0;position:absolute;width:1px}</style><span class="nowrap">4&#160;ft&#160;<span class="frac" role="math">8<span class="sr-only">+</span><span class="num">1</span>&#8260;<span class="den">2</span></span>&#160;in</span>)<a href="/wiki/Standard_gauge" class="mw-redirect" title="Standard gauge">-gauge</a> line from <a href="/wiki/Latour-de-Carol" title="Latour-de-Carol">Latour-de-Carol</a> (25&#160;km or 16&#160;mi) southeast of Andorra, to <a href="/wiki/Toulouse" title="Toulouse">Toulouse</a> and on to Paris by the French <a href="/wiki/TGV" title="TGV">high-speed trains</a>. This line is operated by the <a href="/wiki/SNCF" title="SNCF">SNCF</a>. Latour-de-Carol has a scenic <span class="nowrap">1,000&#160;mm</span> (<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1020198016"/><span class="nowrap">3&#160;ft&#160;<span class="frac" role="math">3<span class="sr-only">+</span><span class="num">3</span>&#8260;<span class="den">8</span></span>&#160;in</span>) <span class="nowrap"><a href="/wiki/Metre_gauge" class="mw-redirect" title="Metre gauge">metre gauge</a></span> <a href="/wiki/Yellow_Train" class="mw-redirect" title="Yellow Train">trainline</a> to <a href="/wiki/Villefranche-de-Conflent" title="Villefranche-de-Conflent">Villefranche-de-Conflent</a>, as well as the SNCF's <span class="nowrap">1,435&#160;mm</span> gauge line connecting to <a href="/wiki/Perpignan" title="Perpignan">Perpignan</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/RENFE" class="mw-redirect" title="RENFE">RENFE's</a> <span class="nowrap">1,668&#160;mm</span> (<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1020198016"/><span class="nowrap">5&#160;ft&#160;<span class="frac" role="math">5<span class="sr-only">+</span><span class="num">21</span>&#8260;<span class="den">32</span></span>&#160;in</span>) <a href="/wiki/Iberian_gauge" class="mw-redirect" title="Iberian gauge">-gauge</a> line to Barcelona.<sup id="cite_ref-202" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-202">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-203" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-203">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup> There are also direct <a href="/wiki/Intercit%C3%A9s#Night_trains" title="Intercités">Intercités de Nuit</a> trains between L'Hospitalet-près-l'Andorre and Paris on certain dates.<sup id="cite_ref-204" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-204">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Media_and_telecommunications">Media and telecommunications</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Andorra&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Media and telecommunications">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Telecommunications_in_Andorra" title="Telecommunications in Andorra">Telecommunications in Andorra</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Andorra_Telecom" title="Andorra Telecom">Andorra Telecom</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:RTVA_logotip.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/RTVA_logotip.png/220px-RTVA_logotip.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="72" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/6/65/RTVA_logotip.png 1.5x" data-file-width="271" data-file-height="89" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:RTVA_logotip.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Radio_i_Televisio_d%27Andorra" class="mw-redirect" title="Radio i Televisio d&#39;Andorra">RTVA</a>, the public service television and radio broadcaster in Andorra</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Andorra_Telecom_2019_logo.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Andorra_Telecom_2019_logo.svg/220px-Andorra_Telecom_2019_logo.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="34" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Andorra_Telecom_2019_logo.svg/330px-Andorra_Telecom_2019_logo.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Andorra_Telecom_2019_logo.svg/440px-Andorra_Telecom_2019_logo.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="80" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Andorra_Telecom_2019_logo.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Andorra_Telecom" title="Andorra Telecom">Andorra Telecom</a>, the national telecom company in Andorra</div></div></div>
<p>In Andorra, mobile and fixed telephone and internet services are operated exclusively by the Andorran national telecommunications company, SOM, also known as <a href="/wiki/Andorra_Telecom" title="Andorra Telecom">Andorra Telecom</a> (STA). The same company also manages the technical infrastructure for national broadcasting of digital television and radio.<sup id="cite_ref-205" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-205">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup> In 2010 Andorra became the first country to provide a direct <a href="/wiki/Optical_fiber" title="Optical fiber">optical fiber</a> link to all homes (<a href="/wiki/FTTx" class="mw-redirect" title="FTTx">FTTH</a>) and businesses.<sup id="cite_ref-206" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-206">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The first commercial radio station to broadcast was <a href="/wiki/Radio_Andorra" title="Radio Andorra">Radio Andorra</a>, which was active from 1939 to 1981.<sup id="cite_ref-207" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-207">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-208" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-208">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-209" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-209">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> On 12 October 1989, the General Council established radio and television as essential public services creating and managing the entity ORTA, becoming on 13 April 2000, in the public company <a href="/wiki/R%C3%A0dio_i_Televisi%C3%B3_d%27Andorra" title="Ràdio i Televisió d&#39;Andorra">Ràdio i Televisió d'Andorra</a> (RTVA).<sup id="cite_ref-210" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-210">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup> In 1990, the public radio was founded on the Radio Nacional d'Andorra. As an autochthonous television channel, there is only the national public television network Andorra Televisió, created in 1995.<sup id="cite_ref-211" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-211">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup> Additional TV and radio stations from Spain and France are available via digital terrestrial television and IPTV.<sup id="cite_ref-212" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-212">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are three national newspapers, <i><a href="/wiki/Diari_d%27Andorra" title="Diari d&#39;Andorra">Diari d'Andorra</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/El_Peri%C3%B2dic_d%27Andorra" title="El Periòdic d&#39;Andorra">El Periòdic d'Andorra</a></i>, and <i><a href="/wiki/Bondia_(newspaper)" title="Bondia (newspaper)">Bondia</a></i> as well as several local newspapers.<sup id="cite_ref-213" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-213">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup> The history of the Andorran press begins in the period between 1917 and 1937 with the appearance of several periodicals papers such as <i>Les Valls d'Andorra</i> (1917), <i>Nova Andorra</i> (1932) and <i>Andorra Agrícola</i> (1933).<sup id="cite_ref-214" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-214">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup> In 1974, the <i>Poble Andorrà</i> became the first regular newspaper in Andorra.<sup id="cite_ref-215" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-215">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup> There is also an <a href="/wiki/Amateur_radio" title="Amateur radio">amateur radio</a> society<sup id="cite_ref-216" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-216">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup> and news agency ANA with independent management.<sup id="cite_ref-217" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-217">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Andorra&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Andorra" title="Culture of Andorra">Culture of Andorra</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Andorra" title="Music of Andorra">Music of Andorra</a></div>
<p>Andorra is home to folk dances like the contrapàs and marratxa, which survive in <a href="/wiki/Sant_Juli%C3%A0_de_L%C3%B2ria" title="Sant Julià de Lòria">Sant Julià de Lòria</a> especially. Andorran folk music has similarities to the music of its neighbours, but is especially <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Catalonia" title="Music of Catalonia">Catalan</a> in character, especially in the presence of dances such as the <a href="/wiki/Sardana" title="Sardana">sardana</a>. Other Andorran folk dances include contrapàs in <a href="/wiki/Andorra_la_Vella" title="Andorra la Vella">Andorra la Vella</a> and Saint Anne's dance in Escaldes-Engordany. Andorra's national holiday is <a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_Meritxell" title="Our Lady of Meritxell">Our Lady of Meritxell</a> Day, 8 September.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_5-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-5">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Among the more important festivals and traditions are the <a href="/wiki/Can%C3%B2lic" title="Canòlic">Canólich Gathering</a> in May, the Roser d'Ordino in July, the Meritxell Day (National Day of Andorra), the Andorra la Vella Fair, the <a href="/wiki/Sant_Jordi" class="mw-redirect" title="Sant Jordi">Sant Jordi Day</a>, the Santa Llúcia Fair, the Festivity from La Candelera to Canillo, the <a href="/wiki/Carnival" title="Carnival">Carnival</a> of Encamp, the sung of caramelles, the Festivity of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Stephen" title="Saint Stephen">Sant Esteve</a> and the Festa del Poble.<sup id="cite_ref-218" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-218">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-219" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-219">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Andorra participated regularly in the <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest" title="Eurovision Song Contest">Eurovision Song Contest</a> between 2004 and 2009, being the only <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_in_the_Eurovision_Song_Contest" title="List of countries in the Eurovision Song Contest">participating country</a> presenting songs in <a href="/wiki/Catalan_language" title="Catalan language">Catalan</a>.
</p><p>In popular folklore, the best-known Andorran legends are the legend of Charlemagne, according to which this Frankish King would have founded the country, the White Lady of <a href="/wiki/Auviny%C3%A0" class="mw-redirect" title="Auvinyà">Auvinyà</a>, the <a href="/wiki/El_buner_d%27Ordino" title="El buner d&#39;Ordino">Buner d'Ordino</a>, the legend of <a href="/wiki/Engolasters" title="Engolasters">Engolasters Lake</a> and the legend of <a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_Meritxell" title="Our Lady of Meritxell">Our Lady of Meritxell</a>.
</p><p>Andorran gastronomy is mainly <a href="/wiki/Catalan_cuisine" title="Catalan cuisine">Catalan</a>, although it has also adopted other elements of <a href="/wiki/French_cuisine" title="French cuisine">French</a> and <a href="/wiki/Italian_cuisine" title="Italian cuisine">Italian</a> cuisines. The cuisine of the country has similar characteristics with the neighbours of <a href="/wiki/Cerdanya" title="Cerdanya">Cerdanya</a> and <a href="/wiki/Alt_Urgell" title="Alt Urgell">Alt Urgell</a>, with whom it has strong cultural ties. Andorra's cuisine is marked by its nature as mountain valleys. Typical dishes of the country are <a href="/wiki/Quince" title="Quince">quince</a> <a href="/wiki/Aioli" title="Aioli">all-i-oli</a>, duck with winter pear, roast lamb with nuts, pork civet, massegada cake, escarole with pears, duck confit and mushrooms, <a href="/wiki/Escudella" title="Escudella">escudella</a>, spinach with raisins and pine nuts, jelly marmalade, stuffed murgues (mushrooms) with pork, <a href="/wiki/Dandelion" class="mw-redirect" title="Dandelion">dandelion</a> salad, and Andorran river <a href="/wiki/Trout" title="Trout">trout</a>. To drink, <a href="/wiki/Mulled_wine" title="Mulled wine">mulled wine</a> and <a href="/wiki/Beer" title="Beer">beer</a> are also popular.<sup id="cite_ref-220" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-220">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup> Some of the dishes are very common in the <a href="/wiki/Alt_Pirineu_i_Aran" title="Alt Pirineu i Aran">mountainous regions of Catalonia</a>, such as <a href="/wiki/Trinxat" title="Trinxat">trinxat</a>, <a href="/wiki/Embutido" title="Embutido">embotits</a>, cooked snails, rice with mushrooms, mountain rice and <a href="/wiki/Mat%C3%B3" title="Mató">mató</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-221" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-221">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Pre-Romanesque" class="mw-redirect" title="Pre-Romanesque">Pre-Romanesque</a> and <a href="/wiki/Romanesque_art" title="Romanesque art">Romanesque art</a> are one of the most important artistic manifestations and characteristics of the Principality. The Romanesque one allows to know the formation of the <a href="/wiki/Parishes_of_Andorra" title="Parishes of Andorra">parochial communities</a>, the relations of (social and political) power and the national culture. There are a total of forty Romanesque churches that stand out as being small austere ornamentation constructions, as well as <a href="/wiki/Romanesque_architecture" title="Romanesque architecture">bridges</a>, fortresses and <a href="/wiki/Manor_houses" class="mw-redirect" title="Manor houses">manor houses</a> of the same period.<sup id="cite_ref-222" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-222">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-223" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-223">&#91;214&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/w/index.php?title=Summer_solstice_fire_festivals&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Summer solstice fire festivals (page does not exist)">Summer solstice fire festivals</a> in the Pyrenees was included as <a href="/wiki/Intangible_cultural_heritage" title="Intangible cultural heritage">UNESCO Intangible cultural heritage</a> in 2015.<sup id="cite_ref-224" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-224">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup> Also the <a href="/wiki/Madriu-Perafita-Claror_Valley" title="Madriu-Perafita-Claror Valley">Madriu-Perafita-Claror Valley</a> became Andorra's first, and to date its only, <a href="/wiki/UNESCO" title="UNESCO">UNESCO</a> <a href="/wiki/World_Heritage_Site" title="World Heritage Site">World Heritage Site</a> in 2004, with a small extension in 2006.<sup id="cite_ref-225" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-225">&#91;216&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-226" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-226">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul class="gallery mw-gallery-packed center">
		<li class="gallerybox" style="width: 161.33333333333px"><div style="width: 161.33333333333px">
			<div class="thumb" style="width: 159.33333333333px;"><div style="margin:0px auto;"><a href="/wiki/File:HPIM0309.JPG" class="image" title="Andorran flag on a balcony, Ordino"><img alt="Andorran flag on a balcony, Ordino" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cd/HPIM0309.JPG/239px-HPIM0309.JPG" decoding="async" width="160" height="120" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cd/HPIM0309.JPG/359px-HPIM0309.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cd/HPIM0309.JPG/478px-HPIM0309.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2848" data-file-height="2144" /></a></div></div>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Andorra&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Sports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Andorra is famous for the practice of <a href="/wiki/Winter_sports" title="Winter sports">winter sports</a>. Andorra has the largest territory of ski slopes in the <a href="/wiki/Pyrenees" title="Pyrenees">Pyrenees</a> (3100 hectares and about 350&#160;km of slopes) and two ski resorts. Grandvalira is the largest and most popular resort. Other popular sports played in Andorra include <a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">football</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rugby_union" title="Rugby union">rugby union</a>, <a href="/wiki/Basketball" title="Basketball">basketball</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Roller_hockey_(quad)" title="Roller hockey (quad)">roller hockey</a>.
</p><p>For <a href="/wiki/Roller_hockey_(quad)" title="Roller hockey (quad)">roller hockey</a>, <a href="/wiki/Andorra_national_roller_hockey_team" title="Andorra national roller hockey team">Andorra</a> usually plays in <a href="/wiki/CERH_European_Roller_Hockey_Championship" class="mw-redirect" title="CERH European Roller Hockey Championship">CERH Euro Cup</a> and in <a href="/wiki/FIRS_Roller_Hockey_World_Cup" class="mw-redirect" title="FIRS Roller Hockey World Cup">FIRS Roller Hockey World Cup</a>. In 2011, Andorra was the host country to the <a href="/wiki/2011_European_League_Final_Eight" class="mw-redirect" title="2011 European League Final Eight">2011 European League Final Eight</a>.
</p><p>The country is represented in association football by the <a href="/wiki/Andorra_national_football_team" title="Andorra national football team">Andorra national football team</a>. The team gained its first competitive win in a European Championship qualifier on 11 October 2019, against Moldova.<sup id="cite_ref-227" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-227">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup> Football is governed in Andorra by the <a href="/wiki/Andorran_Football_Federation" title="Andorran Football Federation">Andorran Football Federation</a> – founded in 1994, it organizes the national competitions of association football (<a href="/wiki/Primera_Divisi%C3%B3" title="Primera Divisió">Primera Divisió</a>, <a href="/wiki/Copa_Constituci%C3%B3" title="Copa Constitució">Copa Constitució</a> and <a href="/wiki/Andorran_Supercup" title="Andorran Supercup">Supercopa</a>) and <a href="/wiki/Futsal" title="Futsal">futsal</a>. Andorra was admitted to <a href="/wiki/UEFA" title="UEFA">UEFA</a> and <a href="/wiki/FIFA" title="FIFA">FIFA</a> in the same year, 1996. <a href="/wiki/FC_Andorra" title="FC Andorra">FC Andorra</a>, a club based in <a href="/wiki/Andorra_la_Vella" title="Andorra la Vella">Andorra la Vella</a> founded in 1942, compete in the <a href="/wiki/Spanish_football_league_system" title="Spanish football league system">Spanish football league system</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Rugby_union" title="Rugby union">Rugby</a> is a traditional sport in Andorra, mainly influenced by the popularity in southern France. The <a href="/wiki/Andorra_national_rugby_union_team" title="Andorra national rugby union team">Andorra national rugby union team</a>, nicknamed Els Isards, plays on the international stage in <a href="/wiki/Rugby_union" title="Rugby union">rugby union</a> and <a href="/wiki/Rugby_sevens" title="Rugby sevens">rugby sevens</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-228" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-228">&#91;219&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/VPC_Andorra_XV" title="VPC Andorra XV">VPC Andorra XV</a> is a rugby team based in Andorra la Vella, which actually plays in the French championship.
</p><p>Basketball popularity has increased in the country since the 1990s, when the Andorran team <a href="/wiki/BC_Andorra" title="BC Andorra">BC Andorra</a> played in the top league of Spain (<a href="/wiki/Liga_ACB" title="Liga ACB">Liga ACB</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-229" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-229">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup> After 18 years the club returned to the top league in 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-230" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-230">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Other sports practised in Andorra include cycling, volleyball, judo, Australian Rules football, handball, swimming, gymnastics, tennis, and motorsports. In 2012, Andorra raised its first national cricket team and played a home match against the Dutch Fellowship of Fairly Odd Places Cricket Club, the first match played in the history of Andorra at an altitude of 1,300 metres (4,300&#160;ft).<sup id="cite_ref-231" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-231">&#91;222&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Andorra first participated at the <a href="/wiki/Olympic_Games" title="Olympic Games">Olympic Games</a> in 1976. The country has appeared in every <a href="/wiki/Winter_Olympic" class="mw-redirect" title="Winter Olympic">Winter Olympic</a> Games since <a href="/wiki/1976_Winter_Olympics" title="1976 Winter Olympics">1976</a>. Andorra competes in the <a href="/wiki/Games_of_the_Small_States_of_Europe" title="Games of the Small States of Europe">Games of the Small States of Europe</a>, being twice the host country, in <a href="/wiki/1991_Games_of_the_Small_States_of_Europe" title="1991 Games of the Small States of Europe">1991</a> and <a href="/wiki/2005_Games_of_the_Small_States_of_Europe" title="2005 Games of the Small States of Europe">2005</a>.
</p><p>As one of the <a href="/wiki/Catalan_Countries" title="Catalan Countries">Catalan Countries</a>, Andorra is home to a team of <a href="/wiki/Castell" title="Castell">castellers</a>, or Catalan human tower builders. The <a href="/w/index.php?title=Castellers_d%27Andorra&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Castellers d&#39;Andorra (page does not exist)">Castellers d'Andorra</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://ca.wikipedia.org/wiki/Castellers_d%27Andorra" class="extiw" title="ca:Castellers d&#39;Andorra">ca</a>&#93;</span>, based in the town of <a href="/wiki/Santa_Coloma_d%27Andorra" title="Santa Coloma d&#39;Andorra">Santa Coloma d'Andorra</a>, are recognized by the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Coordinadora_de_Colles_Castelleres_de_Catalunya&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Coordinadora de Colles Castelleres de Catalunya (page does not exist)">Coordinadora de Colles Castelleres de Catalunya</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://ca.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coordinadora_de_Colles_Castelleres_de_Catalunya" class="extiw" title="ca:Coordinadora de Colles Castelleres de Catalunya">ca</a>&#93;</span>, the governing body of castells.
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Explanatory_notes">Explanatory notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Andorra&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: Explanatory notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-2"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-2">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.andorramania.com/constit_gb.htm">Constitution of Andorra</a>. In Spanish and Portuguese: <span title="Spanish-language text"><i lang="es">Principado de Andorra</i></span>, in French: <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Principauté d'Andorre</i></span>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-4"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-4">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Spanish, Portuguese and French are spoken by a significant minority</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-11"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-11">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><span class="languageicon">(In French)</span> Girard P &amp; Gomez P (2009), <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.lacsdespyrenees.com/vallee-Andorre.html">Lacs des Pyrénées: Andorre</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-15"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-15">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Informe sobre l'estat de la pobresa i la desigualtat al Principal d'Andorra (2003)<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-17"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-17">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Before 1999, the <a href="/wiki/French_franc" title="French franc">French franc</a> and <a href="/wiki/Spanish_peseta" title="Spanish peseta">Spanish peseta</a>; the coins and notes of both currencies, however, remained legal tender until 2002. Small amounts of <a href="/wiki/Andorran_diner" title="Andorran diner">Andorran diners</a> (divided into 100 centim) were minted after 1982.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-19"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-19">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Also <a href="/wiki/.cat" title=".cat">.cat</a>, shared with <a href="/wiki/Pa%C3%AFsos_Catalans" class="mw-redirect" title="Països Catalans">Catalan-speaking territories</a>.</span>
</li>

<li>Waagenaar, Sam, <i>The Little Five</i>. London: Andre Deutsch, 1960.</li></ul>

</div></td></tr></tbody></table><div></div></td></tr><tr><td class="navbox-abovebelow" colspan="3"><div><div class="hlist"><ul><li><sup>1</sup> Spans the conventional boundary between Europe and another continent.</li><li><sup>2</sup> Considered European for cultural, political and historical reasons but is geographically in Western Asia.</li><li><sup>3</sup> <a href="/wiki/Island#Oceanic_islands" title="Island">Oceanic islands</a> within the vicinity of Europe are usually grouped with the continent even though they are not situated on its continental shelf.</li></ul></div>
